1
Ο δάσκαλος ρωτάει ___, όχι εσένα. (us - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο δάσκαλος ρωτάει εμάς, όχι εσένα.
The teacher is asking us, not you. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εσένα

2
Αυτό το δώρο είναι για ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Αυτό το δώρο είναι για εμάς.
This present is for us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as για only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: για μάς

3
Παίρνω τις ___ φωτογραφίες στη δουλειά. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Παίρνω τις δικές μας φωτογραφίες στη δουλειά.
I am taking our own photographs to work. — δικές μας → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative plural to agree with φωτογραφίες, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

4
Παίρνω το ___ δώρο στη δουλειά. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Παίρνω το δικό μας δώρο στη δουλειά.
I am taking our own present to work. — δικό μας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with δώρο, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

5
Παίρνω τα ___ δώρα στη δουλειά. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Παίρνω τα δικά μας δώρα στη δουλειά.
I am taking our own presents to work. — δικά μας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative plural to agree with δώρα, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

19

20
___ αρέσει ο καφές το πρωί. (to us - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Μας αρέσει ο καφές το πρωί.
We like coffee in the morning. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

21
___ τηλεφωνεί κάθε μέρα. (to us - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μας τηλεφωνεί κάθε μέρα.
He phones us every day. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the indirect object of τηλεφωνεί; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

22
___ δίνει τις φωτογραφίες. (to us - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μας δίνει τις φωτογραφίες.
He gives us the photographs. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the indirect object of δίνει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

23
Αυτό είναι το δωμάτιο ___. (our - weak possessive)
Αυτό είναι το δωμάτιο μας.
This is our room. — μας → weak possessive pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here δωμάτιο

24
___ είμαστε στο γραφείο όλη μέρα. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς είμαστε στο γραφείο όλη μέρα.
We are in the office all day. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of είμαστε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

25
___ μένουμε κοντά στο κέντρο. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς μένουμε κοντά στο κέντρο.
We live near the centre. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of μένουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

26
___ αγοράζουμε το πορτοκάλι από την αγορά. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς αγοράζουμε το πορτοκάλι από την αγορά.
We buy the orange at the market. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of αγοράζουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

27
___ αγοράζουμε τα πορτοκάλια από την αγορά. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς αγοράζουμε τα πορτοκάλια από την αγορά.
We buy the oranges at the market. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of αγοράζουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

28
___ μένουμε στο κέντρο; (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς μένουμε στο κέντρο;
Do we live in the centre? — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of μένουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

57
Ο δάσκαλος ρωτάει ___, όχι εμένα. (you (plural) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο δάσκαλος ρωτάει εσάς, όχι εμένα.
The teacher is asking you, not me. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

58
Αυτό το δώρο είναι για ___. (you (plural) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Αυτό το δώρο είναι για εσάς.
This present is for you. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — after a preposition such as για only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: για σάς

59
___ βλέπω κάθε μέρα. (you (plural) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Σας βλέπω κάθε μέρα.
I see you every day. — Σας → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — the direct object of βλέπω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

60

Παίρνω τη ___ βαλίτσα στη δουλειά. (your own (plural) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Παίρνω τη δική σας βαλίτσα στη δουλειά.
I am taking your own suitcase to work. — δική σας → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative singular to agree with βαλίτσα, with the weak genitive σας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Ο δάσκαλος ρωτάει ___, όχι εμένα. (you (singular) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο δάσκαλος ρωτάει εσένα, όχι εμένα.
The teacher is asking you, not me. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

97
Αυτό το δώρο είναι για ___. (you (singular) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Αυτό το δώρο είναι για εσένα.
This present is for you. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — after a preposition such as για only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: για σένα

Do you live in the centre? — Εσύ → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative singular — the subject of μένεις; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

I am taking their own bag to work. — δική τους → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative singular to agree with σακούλα, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

___ αρέσει ο καφές το πρωί. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun with αρέσει)

They like coffee in the morning. — Τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

202
___ τηλεφωνεί κάθε μέρα. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Τους τηλεφωνεί κάθε μέρα.
He phones them every day. — Τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the indirect object of τηλεφωνεί; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

203
___ δίνει τα τηλέφωνα. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Τους δίνει τα τηλέφωνα.
He gives them the telephones. — Τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the indirect object of δίνει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

204
Αυτό είναι το βιβλιοπωλείο ___. (their (masculine) - weak possessive)

This is their bookshop. — τους → weak possessive pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here βιβλιοπωλείο

Αυτοί είναι στο γραφείο όλη μέρα.
They are in the office all day. — Αυτοί → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative plural — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

Παίρνω τα ___ εισιτήρια στη δουλειά. (her own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Παίρνω τα δικά της εισιτήρια στη δουλειά.
I am taking her own tickets to work. — δικά της → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative plural to agree with εισιτήρια, with the weak genitive της for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Το δικό του φαρμακείο είναι κοντά στο κέντρο.
His own pharmacy is near the centre. — δικό του → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative singular to agree with φαρμακείο, with the weak genitive του for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

281
___ αρέσει ο καφές το πρωί. (to him - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Του αρέσει ο καφές το πρωί.
He likes coffee in the morning. — Του → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

282
___ τηλεφωνεί κάθε μέρα. (to him - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Του τηλεφωνεί κάθε μέρα.
He phones him every day. — Του → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the indirect object of τηλεφωνεί; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

283
___ δίνει το κουτί. (to him - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Του δίνει το κουτί.
He gives him the box. — Του → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the indirect object of δίνει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

284
Αυτή είναι η καφετέρια ___. (his - weak possessive)
Αυτή είναι η καφετέρια του.
This is his café. — του → weak possessive pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here καφετέρια

285
___ είναι στο γραφείο όλη μέρα. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός είναι στο γραφείο όλη μέρα.
He is in the office all day. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

286

295
Η εταιρεία διαλέγει ___, όχι εσένα. (us - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Η εταιρεία διαλέγει εμάς, όχι εσένα.
The company is choosing us, not you. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εσένα

296
Αυτό το δώρο είναι από ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Αυτό το δώρο είναι από εμάς.
This present is from us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as από only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: από μάς

Προτιμάω το δικό μας λεμόνι.

They send me a letter. — Μου → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — the indirect object of στέλνουν; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

345
Αυτές είναι οι τσάντες ___. (my - weak possessive)
Αυτές είναι οι τσάντες μου.
These are my handbags. — μου → weak possessive pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here τσάντες

346
___ είμαι από την Ελλάδα. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ είμαι από την Ελλάδα.
I am from Greece. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of είμαι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

347
___ μένω σε μια μικρή πόλη. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ μένω σε μια μικρή πόλη.
I live in a small town. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of μένω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

348
___ αγοράζω ένα πουκάμισο για τον γάμο. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ αγοράζω ένα πουκάμισο για τον γάμο.
I buy a shirt for the wedding. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of αγοράζω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

349

Do I work here? — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of δουλεύω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

351
Η εταιρεία διαλέγει ___, όχι εμένα. (you (plural) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Η εταιρεία διαλέγει εσάς, όχι εμένα.
The company is choosing you, not me. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

352
Αυτό το δώρο είναι από ___. (you (plural) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Αυτό το δώρο είναι από εσάς.
This present is from you. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — after a preposition such as από only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: από σάς

353
___ περιμένω στην πόρτα. (you (plural) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Σας περιμένω στην πόρτα.
I wait for you at the door. — Σας → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — the direct object of περιμένω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

Προτιμάω τα ___ αμύγδαλα. (your own (plural) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Προτιμάω τα δικά σας αμύγδαλα.
I prefer your own almonds. — δικά σας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative plural to agree with αμύγδαλα, with the weak genitive σας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Το δικό σου λεωφορείο είναι στον δρόμο.
Your own bus is in the street. — δικό σου → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative singular to agree with λεωφορείο, with the weak genitive σου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

416
Τα ___ λεωφορεία είναι στον δρόμο. (your own (singular) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Τα δικά σου λεωφορεία είναι στον δρόμο.

Αυτό το καράβι είναι ___. (yours (singular) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)

This boat is yours. — δικό σου → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative singular to agree with καράβι, with the weak genitive σου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

421
___ αρέσει η ελληνική μουσική. (to you (singular) - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Σου αρέσει η ελληνική μουσική.
You like Greek music. — Σου → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person genitive singular — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

422
___ στέλνουν μια συνταγή. (to you (singular) - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Σου στέλνουν μια συνταγή.
They send you a recipe. — Σου → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person genitive singular — the indirect object of στέλνουν; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

423
Αυτά είναι τα κουτιά ___. (your (singular) - weak possessive)
Αυτά είναι τα κουτιά σου.
These are your boxes. — σου → weak possessive pronoun, 2nd person genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here κουτιά

424
___ είσαι από την Ελλάδα. (you (singular) - strong subject pronoun)
Εσύ είσαι από την Ελλάδα.
You are from Greece. — Εσύ → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative singular — the subject of είσαι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

425
___ μένεις σε μια μικρή πόλη. (you (singular) - strong subject pronoun)
Εσύ μένεις σε μια μικρή πόλη.
You live in a small town. — Εσύ → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative singular — the subject of μένεις; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

429
Η εταιρεία διαλέγει ___, όχι εμένα. (them (feminine) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Η εταιρεία διαλέγει αυτές, όχι εμένα.
The company is choosing them, not me. — αυτές → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

430
Αυτό το δώρο είναι από ___. (them (feminine) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Αυτό το δώρο είναι από αυτές.
This present is from them. — αυτές → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative plural — after a preposition such as από only the strong form is possible, never the weak one

431
___ περιμένω στην πόρτα. (them (feminine) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Τις περιμένω στην πόρτα.
I wait for them at the door. — Τις → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative plural — the direct object of περιμένω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

I prefer their own dessert. — δικό τους → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with γλυκό, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

497
___ αρέσει η ελληνική μουσική. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Τους αρέσει η ελληνική μουσική.
They like Greek music. — Τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

498
___ γράφει από την Ελλάδα. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Τους γράφει από την Ελλάδα.
She writes to them from Greece. — Τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the indirect object of γράφει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

579
___ αρέσει η ελληνική μουσική. (to him - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Του αρέσει η ελληνική μουσική.
He likes Greek music. — Του → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

580
___ γράφει από την Ελλάδα. (to him - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Του γράφει από την Ελλάδα.
She writes to him from Greece. — Του → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the indirect object of γράφει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

581
___ στέλνουν ένα κείμενο. (to him - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Του στέλνουν ένα κείμενο.

Αυτοί είναι οι χάρτες του.
These are his maps. — του → weak possessive pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here χάρτες

583
___ είναι από την Ελλάδα. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός είναι από την Ελλάδα.
He is from Greece. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

594

Η δασκάλα μιλάει με εμάς.

My own chair is in the kitchen. — δική μου → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative singular to agree with καρέκλα, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

632
Οι ___ καρέκλες είναι στην κουζίνα. (my own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Οι δικές μου καρέκλες είναι στην κουζίνα.
My own chairs are in the kitchen. — δικές μου → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative plural to agree with καρέκλες, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

633
Το ___ κρεβάτι είναι στην κουζίνα. (my own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Το δικό μου κρεβάτι είναι στην κουζίνα.
My own bed is in the kitchen. — δικό μου → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative singular to agree with κρεβάτι, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

634
Τα ___ κρεβάτια είναι στην κουζίνα. (my own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Τα δικά μου κρεβάτια είναι στην κουζίνα.

635

636
Αυτές οι καρέκλες είναι ___. (mine - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτές οι καρέκλες είναι δικές μου.
These chairs are mine. — δικές μου → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative plural to agree with καρέκλες, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

637
Αυτά τα κρεβάτια είναι ___. (mine - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτά τα κρεβάτια είναι δικά μου.
These beds are mine. — δικά μου → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative plural to agree with κρεβάτια, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

638
Αυτές οι κουρτίνες είναι ___. (mine - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτές οι κουρτίνες είναι δικές μου.
These curtains are mine. — δικές μου → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative plural to agree with κουρτίνες, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

639
Αυτά τα χαλιά είναι ___. (mine - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτά τα χαλιά είναι δικά μου.
These carpets are mine. — δικά μου → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative plural to agree with χαλιά, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

640
___ αρέσουν τα γλυκά. (to me - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Μου αρέσουν τα γλυκά.
I like sweets. — Μου → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

641
___ στέλνουν ένα δώρο κάθε χρόνο. (to me - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μου στέλνουν ένα δώρο κάθε χρόνο.
They send me a present every year. — Μου → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — the indirect object of στέλνουν; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

642
___ φέρνει το φαγητό. (to me - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μου φέρνει το φαγητό.
She brings me the dish. — Μου → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — the indirect object of φέρνει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

643
Πού είναι η τσάντα ___; (my - weak possessive)
Πού είναι η τσάντα μου;
Where is my handbag? — μου → weak possessive pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here τσάντα

644
___ είμαι στην ταβέρνα τώρα. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ είμαι στην ταβέρνα τώρα.
I am at the taverna now. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of είμαι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

645
___ δουλεύω σε ένα μεγάλο γραφείο. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ δουλεύω σε ένα μεγάλο γραφείο.
I work in a big office. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of δουλεύω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

646
___ διαβάζω το βιβλίο κάθε βράδυ. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ διαβάζω το βιβλίο κάθε βράδυ.
I read the book every evening. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of διαβάζω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

647
___ διαβάζω τα βιβλία κάθε βράδυ. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ διαβάζω τα βιβλία κάθε βράδυ.
I read the books every evening. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of διαβάζω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

648
___ έρχομαι στη γιορτή; (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ έρχομαι στη γιορτή;
Am I coming to the party? — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of έρχομαι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

649
Ο γιατρός περιμένει ___, όχι εμένα. (you (plural) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο γιατρός περιμένει εσάς, όχι εμένα.
The doctor is waiting for you, not me. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

650
Η δασκάλα μιλάει με ___. (you (plural) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Η δασκάλα μιλάει με εσάς.
The teacher is talking with you. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — after a preposition such as με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: με σάς

651
___ ξέρω από το σχολείο. (you (plural) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Σας ξέρω από το σχολείο.
I know you from school. — Σας → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — the direct object of ξέρω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

652

I cannot find their own telephones. — δικά τους → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative plural to agree with τηλέφωνα, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

___ είναι καινούρια. (it (feminine) - strong subject pronoun)

845

Ο γιατρός περιμένει αυτόν, όχι εμένα.
The doctor is waiting for him, not me. — αυτόν → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

846
Η δασκάλα μιλάει με ___. (him - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Η δασκάλα μιλάει με αυτόν.
The teacher is talking with him. — αυτόν → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine accusative singular — after a preposition such as με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one

Δεν βρίσκω το ___ κουτί. (his own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)

___ αρέσουν οι μεγάλες πόλεις. (to us - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Μας αρέσουν οι μεγάλες πόλεις.
We like big cities. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

917
___ δίνει τα κλειδιά. (to us - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μας δίνει τα κλειδιά.
He gives us the keys. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the indirect object of δίνει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

918
___ δείχνει τα κάστρα. (to us - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μας δείχνει τα κάστρα.
He shows us the castles. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the indirect object of δείχνει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

919
Δεν βρίσκω τις μάσκες ___. (our - weak possessive)
Δεν βρίσκω τις μάσκες μας.
I cannot find our masks. — μας → weak possessive pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here μάσκες

920
___ είμαστε στο σπίτι το Σαββατοκύριακο. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς είμαστε στο σπίτι το Σαββατοκύριακο.
We are at home at the weekend. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of είμαστε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

Τα παιδιά χαιρετάνε ___, όχι εσένα. (me - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Τα παιδιά χαιρετάνε εμένα, όχι εσένα.
The children are greeting me, not you. — εμένα → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εσένα

I want my own handbag back. — δική μου → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative singular to agree with τσάντα, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Your own magazines are in the library. — δικά σας → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative plural to agree with περιοδικά, with the weak genitive σας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

997
Δεν θέλω να φύγω χωρίς ___. (you (singular) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Δεν θέλω να φύγω χωρίς εσένα.
I do not want to leave without you. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — after a preposition such as χωρίς only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: χωρίς σένα

Θέλω τους δικούς της φακέλους πίσω.
I want her own envelopes back. — δικούς της → emphatic possessive, masculine accusative plural to agree with φακέλους, with the weak genitive της for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Χρησιμοποιώ τις ___ κατσαρόλες κάθε μέρα. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Χρησιμοποιώ τις δικές μας κατσαρόλες κάθε μέρα.
I use our own pots every day. — δικές μας → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative plural to agree with κατσαρόλες, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1191
Χρησιμοποιώ το ___ τηγάνι κάθε μέρα. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Χρησιμοποιώ το δικό μας τηγάνι κάθε μέρα.
I use our own frying pan every day. — δικό μας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with τηγάνι, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Χρησιμοποιώ τα δικά μας τηγάνια κάθε μέρα.

1193

Τα κλειδιά του δικού μας δωματίου είναι στο γραφείο.
The keys of our own room are in the office. — δικού μας → emphatic possessive, neuter genitive singular to agree with δωματίου, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1201
Αυτό το σαλόνι είναι ___. (ours - emphatic δικός-type possessive)

1202
Αυτός ο κήπος είναι ___. (ours - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτός ο κήπος είναι δικός μας.
This garden is ours. — δικός μας → emphatic possessive, masculine nominative singular to agree with κήπος, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1203
___ αρέσει το καλοκαίρι. (to us - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Μας αρέσει το καλοκαίρι.
We like summer. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

1204
___ ετοιμάζει έναν καφέ. (to us - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μας ετοιμάζει έναν καφέ.
She prepares us a coffee. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the indirect object of ετοιμάζει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

1205
___ αγοράζουν ένα φόρεμα. (to us - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μας αγοράζουν ένα φόρεμα.
They buy us a dress. — Μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the indirect object of αγοράζουν; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

1206
Μου αρέσει πολύ το δωμάτιο ___. (our - weak possessive)
Μου αρέσει πολύ το δωμάτιο μας.
I really like our room. — μας → weak possessive pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here δωμάτιο

1207
___ είμαστε στην παραλία όλο το απόγευμα. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς είμαστε στην παραλία όλο το απόγευμα.
We are on the beach all afternoon. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of είμαστε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1208
___ ταξιδεύουμε πολύ τον χειμώνα. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς ταξιδεύουμε πολύ τον χειμώνα.
We travel a lot in the winter. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of ταξιδεύουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1209
___ ετοιμάζουμε το φαγητό για το βράδυ. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς ετοιμάζουμε το φαγητό για το βράδυ.
We prepare the dish for the evening. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of ετοιμάζουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1210
___ ετοιμάζουμε τα φαγητά για το βράδυ. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς ετοιμάζουμε τα φαγητά για το βράδυ.
We prepare the dishes for the evening. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of ετοιμάζουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1211
___ ξέρουμε τη διεύθυνση; (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς ξέρουμε τη διεύθυνση;
Do we know the address? — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of ξέρουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1212
Η αστυνομία ψάχνει ___, όχι εσένα. (me - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Η αστυνομία ψάχνει εμένα, όχι εσένα.
The police are looking for me, not you. — εμένα → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εσένα

1213
Ο Νίκος έρχεται στη γιορτή μαζί με ___. (me - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Ο Νίκος έρχεται στη γιορτή μαζί με εμένα.
Nikos is coming to the party together with me. — εμένα → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative singular — after a preposition such as μαζί με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: μαζί με μένα

1214
___ περιμένει στο σπίτι. (me - weak pronoun, direct object)
Με περιμένει στο σπίτι.
He waits for me at the house. — Με → weak personal pronoun, 1st person accusative singular — the direct object of περιμένει; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1215

He waits for me at the kitchen. — Με → weak personal pronoun, 1st person accusative singular — the direct object of περιμένει; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1249

The police are looking for you, not me. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

I use your own knife every day. — δικό σας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with μαχαίρι, with the weak genitive σας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Η αστυνομία ψάχνει εσένα, όχι εμένα.
The police are looking for you, not me. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

1285
Ο Νίκος έρχεται στη γιορτή μαζί με ___. (you (singular) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Ο Νίκος έρχεται στη γιορτή μαζί με εσένα.
Nikos is coming to the party together with you. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — after a preposition such as μαζί με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: μαζί με σένα

Χρησιμοποιώ το ___ καθαριστικό κάθε μέρα. (their own (feminine) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Χρησιμοποιώ το δικό τους καθαριστικό κάθε μέρα.
I use their own cleaning product every day. — δικό τους → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with καθαριστικό, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Nikos is coming to the party together with them. — αυτούς → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine accusative plural — after a preposition such as μαζί με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one

I don't find them anywhere. — τα → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter accusative plural — the direct object of βρίσκω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1389
___ είναι στο δωμάτιο. (they (neuter) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτά είναι στο δωμάτιο.
They are in the room. — Αυτά → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter nominative plural — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1390
___ είναι μεγάλα. (they (neuter) - strong subject pronoun)

1391

Η αστυνομία ψάχνει αυτήν, όχι εμένα.
The police are looking for her, not me. — αυτήν → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

1392
Ο Νίκος έρχεται στη γιορτή μαζί με ___. (her - strong pronoun after a preposition)

Nikos is coming to the party together with her. — αυτήν → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative singular — after a preposition such as μαζί με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one

1403
Χρησιμοποιώ τις ___ συσκευές κάθε μέρα. (her own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Χρησιμοποιώ τις δικές της συσκευές κάθε μέρα.
I use her own appliances every day. — δικές της → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative plural to agree with συσκευές, with the weak genitive της for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1460
Δεν ___ βρίσκω πουθενά. (it (neuter) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Δεν το βρίσκω πουθενά.
I don't find it anywhere. — το → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter accusative singular — the direct object of βρίσκω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1461
___ είναι στο δωμάτιο. (it (neuter) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτό είναι στο δωμάτιο.
It is in the room. — Αυτό → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1462
___ είναι μεγάλο. (it (neuter) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτό είναι μεγάλο.
It is big. — Αυτό → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1463
Οι γείτονες κοιτάζουν ___, όχι εσένα. (us - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Οι γείτονες κοιτάζουν εμάς, όχι εσένα.
The neighbours are looking at us, not you. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εσένα

1464
Το γράμμα είναι για ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Το γράμμα είναι για εμάς.
The letter is for us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as για only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: για μάς

1465

Μας βλέπουν στο μνημείο.

1474
Αγοράζω τους ___ σκούφους από την αγορά. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αγοράζω τους δικούς μας σκούφους από την αγορά.
I am buying our own woolly hats at the market. — δικούς μας → emphatic possessive, masculine accusative plural to agree with σκούφους, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Αυτά τα άρθρα δεν είναι ___. (mine - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτά τα άρθρα δεν είναι δικά μου.
These articles are not mine. — δικά μου → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative plural to agree with άρθρα, with the weak genitive μου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1528
___ αρέσουν τα ταξίδια. (to me - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Μου αρέσουν τα ταξίδια.
I like travelling. — Μου → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

1529
___ φέρνουν λουλούδια. (to me - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μου φέρνουν λουλούδια.
They bring me flowers. — Μου → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — the indirect object of φέρνουν; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

1530
___ ετοιμάζει το φαγητό. (to me - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Μου ετοιμάζει το φαγητό.
She prepares me the dish. — Μου → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — the indirect object of ετοιμάζει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

1531
Περιμένω τον φίλο ___ στην είσοδο. (my - weak possessive)
Περιμένω τον φίλο μου στην είσοδο.
I am waiting for my friend at the entrance. — μου → weak possessive pronoun, 1st person genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here φίλο

1532
___ είμαι εδώ κάθε πρωί. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ είμαι εδώ κάθε πρωί.
I am here every morning. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of είμαι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1533
___ ξυπνάω πολύ νωρίς κάθε πρωί. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ ξυπνάω πολύ νωρίς κάθε πρωί.
I wake up very early every morning. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of ξυπνάω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1534
___ μαγειρεύω το φαγητό κάθε Κυριακή. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ μαγειρεύω το φαγητό κάθε Κυριακή.
I cook the dish every Sunday. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of μαγειρεύω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1535
___ μαγειρεύω τα φαγητά κάθε Κυριακή. (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ μαγειρεύω τα φαγητά κάθε Κυριακή.
I cook the dishes every Sunday. — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of μαγειρεύω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1536
___ πηγαίνω στη συναυλία; (I - strong subject pronoun)
Εγώ πηγαίνω στη συναυλία;
Am I going to the concert? — Εγώ → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative singular — the subject of πηγαίνω; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1537
Οι γείτονες κοιτάζουν ___, όχι εμένα. (you (plural) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Οι γείτονες κοιτάζουν εσάς, όχι εμένα.
The neighbours are looking at you, not me. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

1538
Το γράμμα είναι για ___. (you (plural) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Το γράμμα είναι για εσάς.
The letter is for you. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — after a preposition such as για only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: για σάς

1539
___ συμπαθώ πολύ. (you (plural) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Σας συμπαθώ πολύ.
I like you a lot. — Σας → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — the direct object of συμπαθώ; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1540
___ βλέπουν στην αυλή. (you (plural) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Σας βλέπουν στην αυλή.
They see you at the yard. — Σας → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — the direct object of βλέπουν; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1548
Αγοράζω τη ___ μάσκα από την αγορά. (your own (plural) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αγοράζω τη δική σας μάσκα από την αγορά.
I am buying your own mask at the market. — δική σας → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative singular to agree with μάσκα, with the weak genitive σας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Your own telephones are in the office. — δικά σου → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative plural to agree with τηλέφωνα, with the weak genitive σου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1594

Your own computer is in the office. — δικός σου → emphatic possessive, masculine nominative singular to agree with υπολογιστής, with the weak genitive σου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1600
___ ετοιμάζει τις σαλάτες. (to you (singular) - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Σου ετοιμάζει τις σαλάτες.
She prepares you the salads. — Σου → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person genitive singular — the indirect object of ετοιμάζει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

1601
Περιμένω τον θείο ___ στην είσοδο. (your (singular) - weak possessive)
Περιμένω τον θείο σου στην είσοδο.
I am waiting for your uncle at the entrance. — σου → weak possessive pronoun, 2nd person genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here θείο

1602
___ είσαι εδώ κάθε πρωί. (you (singular) - strong subject pronoun)
Εσύ είσαι εδώ κάθε πρωί.
You are here every morning. — Εσύ → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative singular — the subject of είσαι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1603

1607
Οι γείτονες κοιτάζουν ___, όχι εμένα. (them (feminine) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Οι γείτονες κοιτάζουν αυτές, όχι εμένα.
The neighbours are looking at them, not me. — αυτές → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

1608

I like them a lot. — Τις → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative plural — the direct object of συμπαθώ; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

Οι δικοί τους υπολογιστές είναι στο γραφείο.
Their own computers are in the office. — δικοί τους → emphatic possessive, masculine nominative plural to agree with υπολογιστές, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1660

These messages are not theirs. — δικά τους → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative plural to agree with μηνύματα, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1664
___ αρέσουν τα ταξίδια. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Τους αρέσουν τα ταξίδια.
They like travelling. — Τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

1665
___ φέρνουν λουλούδια. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Τους φέρνουν λουλούδια.
They bring them flowers. — Τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the indirect object of φέρνουν; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

1666
Περιμένω την αδερφή ___ στην είσοδο. (their (masculine) - weak possessive)
Περιμένω την αδερφή τους στην είσοδο.
I am waiting for their sister at the entrance. — τους → weak possessive pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here αδερφή

1667
___ είναι εδώ κάθε πρωί. (they (masculine) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτοί είναι εδώ κάθε πρωί.
They are here every morning. — Αυτοί → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative plural — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

Αυτοί ξυπνάνε πολύ νωρίς κάθε πρωί.

Οι δικές του σακούλες είναι στο γραφείο.
His own bags are in the office. — δικές του → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative plural to agree with σακούλες, with the weak genitive του for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1731
Αυτές οι προσκλήσεις δεν είναι ___. (his - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτές οι προσκλήσεις δεν είναι δικές του.
These invitations are not his. — δικές του → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative plural to agree with προσκλήσεις, with the weak genitive του for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1732
Αυτές οι αγγελίες δεν είναι ___. (his - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτές οι αγγελίες δεν είναι δικές του.
These advertisements are not his. — δικές του → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative plural to agree with αγγελίες, with the weak genitive του for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1733
Αυτές οι διαφημίσεις δεν είναι ___. (his - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτές οι διαφημίσεις δεν είναι δικές του.
These advertisements are not his. — δικές του → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative plural to agree with διαφημίσεις, with the weak genitive του for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1734
___ αρέσουν τα ταξίδια. (to him - weak pronoun with αρέσει)
Του αρέσουν τα ταξίδια.
He likes travelling. — Του → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — with αρέσει the person who likes something stands in the genitive and the thing liked is the subject

1735
___ φέρνουν λουλούδια. (to him - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Του φέρνουν λουλούδια.
They bring him flowers. — Του → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the indirect object of φέρνουν; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

1736
Περιμένω την εγγονή ___ στην είσοδο. (his - weak possessive)
Περιμένω την εγγονή του στην είσοδο.
I am waiting for his granddaughter at the entrance. — του → weak possessive pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here εγγονή

1737
___ είναι εδώ κάθε πρωί. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός είναι εδώ κάθε πρωί.
He is here every morning. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1738
___ ξυπνάει πολύ νωρίς κάθε πρωί. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός ξυπνάει πολύ νωρίς κάθε πρωί.
He wakes up very early every morning. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of ξυπνάει; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1739
___ μαγειρεύει το μεσημεριανό κάθε Κυριακή. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός μαγειρεύει το μεσημεριανό κάθε Κυριακή.
He cooks the lunch every Sunday. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of μαγειρεύει; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1740
___ είναι ελεύθερος. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός είναι ελεύθερος.
He is free. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1741
___ πηγαίνει στη συναυλία; (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός πηγαίνει στη συναυλία;
Is he going to the concert? — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of πηγαίνει; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1742
___ είναι εδώ από χθες. (it (masculine) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός είναι εδώ από χθες.
It is here since yesterday. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1743
___ είναι μικρός. (it (masculine) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός είναι μικρός.
It is small. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1744
___ καθαρίζουμε κάθε Σάββατο. (it (neuter) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Το καθαρίζουμε κάθε Σάββατο.
We clean it every Saturday. — Το → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter accusative singular — the direct object of καθαρίζουμε; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1745
___ είναι εδώ από χθες. (it (neuter) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτό είναι εδώ από χθες.
It is here since yesterday. — Αυτό → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1746
___ είναι μικρό. (it (neuter) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτό είναι μικρό.
It is small. — Αυτό → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1747
Ο σκηνοθέτης διαλέγει ___, όχι εσένα. (us - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο σκηνοθέτης διαλέγει εμάς, όχι εσένα.
The director is choosing us, not you. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εσένα

1748
Το γράμμα είναι από ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Το γράμμα είναι από εμάς.
The letter is from us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as από only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: από μάς

1749
Καθαρίζω το ___ γραφείο κάθε Σάββατο. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Καθαρίζω το δικό μας γραφείο κάθε Σάββατο.
I clean our own office every Saturday. — δικό μας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with γραφείο, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1750
Καθαρίζω τη ___ αίθουσα κάθε Σάββατο. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Καθαρίζω τη δική μας αίθουσα κάθε Σάββατο.
I clean our own hall every Saturday. — δική μας → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative singular to agree with αίθουσα, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1751

Καθαρίζω τη δική μας αποθήκη κάθε Σάββατο.
I clean our own storeroom every Saturday. — δική μας → emphatic possessive, feminine accusative singular to agree with αποθήκη, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Καθαρίζω το ___ διαμέρισμα κάθε Σάββατο. (your own (plural) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Καθαρίζω το δικό σας διαμέρισμα κάθε Σάββατο.
I clean your own flat every Saturday. — δικό σας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with διαμέρισμα, with the weak genitive σας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1800
Έρχονται οι μητέρες ___ στο σπίτι. (your (plural) - weak possessive)
Έρχονται οι μητέρες σας στο σπίτι.
Your mothers are coming to the house. — σας → weak possessive pronoun, 2nd person genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here μητέρες

1801
___ είστε στην ίδια τάξη. (you (plural) - strong subject pronoun)
Εσείς είστε στην ίδια τάξη.
You are in the same class. — Εσείς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative plural — the subject of είστε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1802
___ περπατάτε στην παραλία κάθε απόγευμα. (you (plural) - strong subject pronoun)
Εσείς περπατάτε στην παραλία κάθε απόγευμα.

1806
Ο σκηνοθέτης διαλέγει ___, όχι εμένα. (you (singular) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο σκηνοθέτης διαλέγει εσένα, όχι εμένα.
The director is choosing you, not me. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

1807
Το γράμμα είναι από ___. (you (singular) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Το γράμμα είναι από εσένα.
The letter is from you. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — after a preposition such as από only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: από σένα

1808
___ ψάχνω όλο το πρωί. (you (singular) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Σε ψάχνω όλο το πρωί.
I look for you all morning. — Σε → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — the direct object of ψάχνω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1876
Καθαρίζω το ___ ισόγειο κάθε Σάββατο. (their own (masculine) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Καθαρίζω το δικό τους ισόγειο κάθε Σάββατο.
I clean their own ground floor every Saturday. — δικό τους → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with ισόγειο, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1877

1884

1885

Καθαρίζω το ___ σπίτι κάθε Σάββατο. (his own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Καθαρίζω το δικό του σπίτι κάθε Σάββατο.
I clean his own house every Saturday. — δικό του → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with σπίτι, with the weak genitive του for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

1943
___ εξηγεί την άσκηση. (to him - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Του εξηγεί την άσκηση.
He explains the exercise to him. — Του → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the indirect object of εξηγεί; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

1944
Έρχονται οι εγγονές ___ στο σπίτι. (his - weak possessive)
Έρχονται οι εγγονές του στο σπίτι.
His granddaughters are coming to the house. — του → weak possessive pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive singular — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here εγγονές

1945
___ είναι στην ίδια τάξη. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός είναι στην ίδια τάξη.
He is in the same class. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1946
___ περπατάει στην παραλία κάθε απόγευμα. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτός περπατάει στην παραλία κάθε απόγευμα.
He walks on the beach every afternoon. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of περπατάει; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

Αυτός πλένει την πετσέτα μετά το φαγητό.
He washes the towel after the meal. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of πλένει; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

Is he driving today? — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of οδηγάει; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

It is clean. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1951
___ χρησιμοποιώ κάθε μέρα. (it (neuter) - weak pronoun, direct object)

I use it every day. — Το → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter accusative singular — the direct object of χρησιμοποιώ; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

1952
___ είναι καθαρό. (it (neuter) - strong subject pronoun)

It is clean. — Αυτό → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter nominative singular — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

1953
Η δασκάλα βοηθάει ___, όχι εσένα. (us - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Η δασκάλα βοηθάει εμάς, όχι εσένα.
The teacher is helping us, not you. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εσένα

1963
Οδηγάω το ___ λεωφορείο με προσοχή. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Οδηγάω το δικό μας λεωφορείο με προσοχή.
I drive our own bus carefully. — δικό μας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with λεωφορείο, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

Αυτοί οι σκούφοι είναι ___. (yours (plural) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτοί οι σκούφοι είναι δικοί σας.
These woolly hats are yours. — δικοί σας → emphatic possessive, masculine nominative plural to agree with σκούφοι, with the weak genitive σας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2016
Αυτές οι φόρμες είναι ___. (yours (plural) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτές οι φόρμες είναι δικές σας.
These tracksuits are yours. — δικές σας → emphatic possessive, feminine nominative plural to agree with φόρμες, with the weak genitive σας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2019
Καθαρίζω την κουζίνα ___ κάθε Σάββατο. (your (plural) - weak possessive)
Καθαρίζω την κουζίνα σας κάθε Σάββατο.
I clean your kitchen every Saturday. — σας → weak possessive pronoun, 2nd person genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here κουζίνα

2020
___ είστε στη φωτογραφία. (you (plural) - strong subject pronoun)
Εσείς είστε στη φωτογραφία.
You are in the photograph. — Εσείς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative plural — the subject of είστε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2025
Η δασκάλα βοηθάει ___, όχι εμένα. (you (singular) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Η δασκάλα βοηθάει εσένα, όχι εμένα.
The teacher is helping you, not me. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

2026
Θα έρθω στο θέατρο με ___. (you (singular) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Θα έρθω στο θέατρο με εσένα.
I will come to the theatre with you. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — after a preposition such as με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: με σένα

2027
___ βοηθάω με τις ασκήσεις. (you (singular) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Σε βοηθάω με τις ασκήσεις.
I help you with the exercises. — Σε → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — the direct object of βοηθάω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

I drive your own car carefully. — δικό σου → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with αυτοκίνητο, with the weak genitive σου for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

I drive their own bus carefully. — δικό τους → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with λεωφορείο, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2087
Οδηγάω το ___ ποδήλατο με προσοχή. (their own (masculine) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Οδηγάω το δικό τους ποδήλατο με προσοχή.
I drive their own bicycle carefully. — δικό τους → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with ποδήλατο, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2088
Αυτά τα σακάκια είναι ___. (theirs (masculine) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτά τα σακάκια είναι δικά τους.
These jackets are theirs. — δικά τους → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative plural to agree with σακάκια, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2089
Αυτά τα κοστούμια είναι ___. (theirs (masculine) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτά τα κοστούμια είναι δικά τους.
These suits are theirs. — δικά τους → emphatic possessive, neuter nominative plural to agree with κοστούμια, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2090
Αυτοί οι σκούφοι είναι ___. (theirs (masculine) - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Αυτοί οι σκούφοι είναι δικοί τους.
These woolly hats are theirs. — δικοί τους → emphatic possessive, masculine nominative plural to agree with σκούφοι, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2091
___ δείχνει τις φωτογραφίες. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Τους δείχνει τις φωτογραφίες.
She shows them the photographs. — Τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the indirect object of δείχνει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

2092
Καθαρίζω το ισόγειο ___ κάθε Σάββατο. (their (masculine) - weak possessive)
Καθαρίζω το ισόγειο τους κάθε Σάββατο.
I clean their ground floor every Saturday. — τους → weak possessive pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here ισόγειο

2093
___ είναι στη φωτογραφία. (they (masculine) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτοί είναι στη φωτογραφία.
They are in the photograph. — Αυτοί → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative plural — the subject of είναι; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2094

Αυτοί τρέχουν στο γυμναστήριο κάθε μέρα.

2101
Η δασκάλα βοηθάει ___, όχι εμένα. (her - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Η δασκάλα βοηθάει αυτήν, όχι εμένα.
The teacher is helping her, not me. — αυτήν → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

2102
Θα έρθω στο θέατρο με ___. (her - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Θα έρθω στο θέατρο με αυτήν.
I will come to the theatre with her. — αυτήν → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative singular — after a preposition such as με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one

Διαβάζω το δικό μας βιβλίο κάθε βράδυ.
I read our own book every evening. — δικό μας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative singular to agree with βιβλίο, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2160
Διαβάζω τα ___ βιβλία κάθε βράδυ. (our own - emphatic δικός-type possessive)
Διαβάζω τα δικά μας βιβλία κάθε βράδυ.
I read our own books every evening. — δικά μας → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative plural to agree with βιβλία, with the weak genitive μας for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2161
Δεν ___ απαντάει ποτέ. (to us - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Δεν μας απαντάει ποτέ.
He never answers us. — μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the indirect object of απαντάει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

2162
Πού είναι οι μάσκες ___; (our - weak possessive)
Πού είναι οι μάσκες μας;
Where are our masks? — μας → weak possessive pronoun, 1st person genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here μάσκες

2163
___ μαγειρεύουμε πολύ καλά. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς μαγειρεύουμε πολύ καλά.
We cook very well. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of μαγειρεύουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2164
___ ψάχνουμε τις φωτογραφίες κάθε πρωί. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς ψάχνουμε τις φωτογραφίες κάθε πρωί.
We look for the photographs every morning. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of ψάχνουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2165
___ ψάχνουμε το δώρο κάθε πρωί. (we - strong subject pronoun)
Εμείς ψάχνουμε το δώρο κάθε πρωί.
We look for the present every morning. — Εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of ψάχνουμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2166
Ο καλλιτέχνης ζωγραφίζει ___, όχι εσένα. (me - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο καλλιτέχνης ζωγραφίζει εμένα, όχι εσένα.
The artist is painting me, not you. — εμένα → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εσένα

2179
Ο καλλιτέχνης ζωγραφίζει ___, όχι εμένα. (you (plural) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο καλλιτέχνης ζωγραφίζει εσάς, όχι εμένα.
The artist is painting you, not me. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

2180
Θα έρθω στο θέατρο μαζί με ___. (you (plural) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Θα έρθω στο θέατρο μαζί με εσάς.
I will come to the theatre together with you. — εσάς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — after a preposition such as μαζί με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: μαζί με σάς

2181

I listen to you very well. — Σας → weak personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative plural — the direct object of ακούω; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

Διαβάζω τα δικά τους κείμενα κάθε βράδυ.
I read their own texts every evening. — δικά τους → emphatic possessive, neuter accusative plural to agree with κείμενα, with the weak genitive τους for the owner — δικός/δική/δικό takes the gender, case and number of the thing owned, and the clitic says whose it is

2230
Δεν ___ απαντάει ποτέ. (to them (masculine) - weak pronoun, indirect object)
Δεν τους απαντάει ποτέ.
He never answers them. — τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the indirect object of απαντάει; Greek puts the person who receives in the genitive, in front of the verb

2231
Πού είναι οι κάρτες ___; (their (masculine) - weak possessive)
Πού είναι οι κάρτες τους;
Where are their cards? — τους → weak possessive pronoun, 3rd person masculine genitive plural — the possessive clitic follows the noun it belongs to, here κάρτες

2232
___ μαγειρεύουν πολύ καλά. (they (masculine) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτοί μαγειρεύουν πολύ καλά.
They cook very well. — Αυτοί → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative plural — the subject of μαγειρεύουν; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2233
___ ψάχνουν τα τηλέφωνα κάθε πρωί. (they (masculine) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτοί ψάχνουν τα τηλέφωνα κάθε πρωί.

2234
___ πουλάνε σε καλή τιμή. (them (neuter) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Τα πουλάνε σε καλή τιμή.
They sell them at a good price. — Τα → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person neuter accusative plural — the direct object of πουλάνε; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

2235
Ο καλλιτέχνης ζωγραφίζει ___, όχι εμένα. (her - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο καλλιτέχνης ζωγραφίζει αυτήν, όχι εμένα.
The artist is painting her, not me. — αυτήν → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative singular — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

2236
Θα έρθω στο θέατρο μαζί με ___. (her - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Θα έρθω στο θέατρο μαζί με αυτήν.
I will come to the theatre together with her. — αυτήν → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative singular — after a preposition such as μαζί με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one

2264
Η ζωή είναι δύσκολη χωρίς ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Η ζωή είναι δύσκολη χωρίς εμάς.
Life is difficult without us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as χωρίς only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: χωρίς μάς

They wear the woolly hat to work. — Αυτές → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine nominative plural — the subject of φοράνε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2293
Ο δήμαρχος καλεί ___, όχι εμένα. (them (masculine) - strong pronoun, emphatic object)
Ο δήμαρχος καλεί αυτούς, όχι εμένα.
The mayor is inviting them, not me. — αυτούς → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine accusative plural — a weak pronoun cannot carry a contrast, so the strong one stands after the verb, against εμένα

2294
Η ζωή είναι δύσκολη χωρίς ___. (them (masculine) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Η ζωή είναι δύσκολη χωρίς αυτούς.
Life is difficult without them. — αυτούς → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine accusative plural — after a preposition such as χωρίς only the strong form is possible, never the weak one

You use the washing machines every day. — Εσύ → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative singular — the subject of χρησιμοποιείς; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2598
___ χρησιμοποιείς την κατσαρόλα κάθε μέρα. (you (singular) - strong subject pronoun)
Εσύ χρησιμοποιείς την κατσαρόλα κάθε μέρα.
You use the pot every day. — Εσύ → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative singular — the subject of χρησιμοποιείς; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2599
___ περιμένουν στον σταθμό. (them (feminine) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Τις περιμένουν στον σταθμό.
They wait for them at the station. — Τις → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine accusative plural — the direct object of περιμένουν; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

2611
Η γιαγιά μαγειρεύει για ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Η γιαγιά μαγειρεύει για εμάς.
Grandmother is cooking for us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as για only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: για μάς

He eats at home usually. — Αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of τρώει; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

Το λεωφορείο φεύγει χωρίς ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Το λεωφορείο φεύγει χωρίς εμάς.
The bus is leaving without us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as χωρίς only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: χωρίς μάς

You need a card for the lesson. — Εσείς → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person nominative plural — the subject of χρειάζεστε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

Τα παιδιά παίζουν με ___ στην αυλή. (you (singular) - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Τα παιδιά παίζουν με εσένα στην αυλή.
The children are playing with you in the yard. — εσένα → strong personal pronoun, 2nd person accusative singular — after a preposition such as με only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: με σένα

Οι γονείς μένουν σε ένα σπίτι, αλλά αυτές μένουν σε ένα διαμέρισμα.
The parents live in a house, but they live in a flat. — αυτές → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine nominative plural — the subject of μένουν; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2825
___ σερβίρουν τον χυμό στους πελάτες. (they (feminine) - strong subject pronoun)
Αυτές σερβίρουν τον χυμό στους πελάτες.
They serve the juice to the customers. — Αυτές → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person feminine nominative plural — the subject of σερβίρουν; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2826
Δεν ___ θυμάμαι από το σχολείο. (them (masculine) - weak pronoun, direct object)
Δεν τους θυμάμαι από το σχολείο.
I don't remember them from school. — τους → weak personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine accusative plural — the direct object of θυμάμαι; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

2827
Οι γονείς μένουν σε ένα σπίτι, αλλά ___ μένουν σε ένα διαμέρισμα. (they (masculine) - strong subject pronoun)
Οι γονείς μένουν σε ένα σπίτι, αλλά αυτοί μένουν σε ένα διαμέρισμα.
The parents live in a house, but they live in a flat. — αυτοί → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative plural — the subject of μένουν; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2828

2834

2835
Ο Νίκος δεν ταξιδεύει ποτέ χωρίς ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Ο Νίκος δεν ταξιδεύει ποτέ χωρίς εμάς.
Nikos never travels without us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as χωρίς only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: χωρίς μάς

2836
Δεν ___ περιμένουν σήμερα. (us - weak pronoun, direct object)

Maria drinks coffee, but we prefer juice. — εμείς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person nominative plural — the subject of προτιμάμε; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2960
Οι συνάδελφοι πηγαίνουν στο γραφείο, αλλά ___ δουλεύει από το σπίτι. (he - strong subject pronoun)
Οι συνάδελφοι πηγαίνουν στο γραφείο, αλλά αυτός δουλεύει από το σπίτι.
The colleagues go to the office, but he works from home. — αυτός → strong personal pronoun, 3rd person masculine nominative singular — the subject of δουλεύει; Greek leaves the subject pronoun out unless it is emphatic

2961
Το παιδί δεν φεύγει ποτέ χωρίς ___. (us - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Το παιδί δεν φεύγει ποτέ χωρίς εμάς.
The child never leaves without us. — εμάς → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — after a preposition such as χωρίς only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: χωρίς μάς

2962
Οι φίλες ___ βοηθάνε πάντα. (us - weak pronoun, direct object)
Οι φίλες μας βοηθάνε πάντα.
The friends help us always. — μας → weak personal pronoun, 1st person accusative plural — the direct object of βοηθάνε; the weak form goes in front of the verb, never after it

2963
Το παιδί δεν φεύγει ποτέ χωρίς ___. (me - strong pronoun after a preposition)
Το παιδί δεν φεύγει ποτέ χωρίς εμένα.
The child never leaves without me. — εμένα → strong personal pronoun, 1st person accusative singular — after a preposition such as χωρίς only the strong form is possible, never the weak one; in speech the first ε often drops: χωρίς μένα

2964
Οι φίλες ___ βοηθάνε πάντα. (me - weak pronoun, direct object)
Οι φίλες με βοηθάνε πάντα.

2965
